TYC 8137-2609-1=CD-46 3212.Indicated as a K0V star in
Simbad, it is a field star according toBANYANΣ. It has a
strong lithium in SACY ( EW(Li)=300 mÅ) and is also
X-ray bright, implying an age around 20 Myr. However, the
parallax fromGaia(2.43±0.01 mas) is incompatible with a
dwarf star.Gaia’s RV differs by 25 km/s from the SACY one,
and there is a large∆µsignature between Tycho2 and Gaia
DR3 (∆µ=8.6±3.2 mas/yr), confirming that the star is a
spectroscopic binary. On the other hand, astrometric indica-
tors in Gaia DR3 do not support binarity. Photometric peri-
ods of 0.843 d and 1.224 d have been reported in the litera-
ture (Kiraga 2012; Richards et al. 2012, respectively). One of
the two is the alias of the true period. Our analysis of ASAS
time series favorsP=1.224 d as the true one. The rotation
period coupled with thevsini=160 km/s (SACY) indicate a
radiusR ⋆ >2.6R ⊙, which impliesL ⋆ >3.8L ⊙ andd>370
pc, a value compatible with theGaiaparallax. Isochrone fit-
ting yieldst ⋆ =3.7±1.2 Gyr,M ⋆ =1.35±0.05M ⊙. We
conclude that the system is an evolved star. The very fast
rotation and X-ray emission can be explained if the system
is a tidally-locked system. The high lithium content is also
observed in other RS CvN systems (Pallavicini et al. 1992).
The tidally-locked companion is too close to produce de-
tectable∆µsignatures, possibly hinting at a tertiary com-
ponent. However, this possible additional component is not
seen in SPHERE images.
TYC 8491-1376-1.K4IV star, whichBANYANΣlabels as a field
star. The extremely large value of the RUWE (3.27, corre-
sponding to (S/N) RUWE =21) argues for the presence of an
unresolved stellar companion. An age of 30 Myr was inferred
by Žerjal et al. (2017) based on the CaII infrared triplet. The
age was confirmed by isochronal analysis, assuming that the
system is composed by two stars withq∼1. The star is
listed in the GaiaNSS catalog, with a binary period of 118
d. An equal-mass binary witha≈0.5 au is fully consistent
withGaiaPMEXmaps.
C.2. Old stars
HIP 39826=BD+21 1764.K7V star with an M-type CMS,
BD+21 1764B, at 10”=180 au), which is itself a tight
triple; SB2 from (Shkolnik et al. 2010) and close visual (ρ
0.33"−0.97" from 2012 to 2021, Cortés-Contreras et al.
2017; Tokovinin et al. 2022). Both chromospheric activity
(logR ′
HK =−4.80 dex (Brown et al. 2022) and isochrones
indicate an age between 2 and 4 Gyr. The prominent X-ray
emission might be explained by the contribution of the four
components and possibly by tidal locking of the SB2 (the or-
bital period is not known). Therefore, we did not consider it
for age determination.
HIP 73666.F3IV star (Houk 1982), classified as a Sco-Cen
member in early studies (e.g. de Zeeuw et al. 1999) but re-
jected byBANYANΣ(p<1%). A significant reddeningE(B−
V)=0.12-0.15 mag is inferred by Casagrande et al. (2011)
and Pecaut et al. (2012), and is confirmed in the recent re-
analysis by (Paunzen et al. 2024) based on several photomet-
ric indices. Assuming this reddening, the isochronal anal-
ysis yields eithert ⋆ =5±1 Myr (pre-MS solution) or
t⋆ =1300±100 Myr (post-MS solution). No evidence was
found for the existence of a CMS group related to the star.
We considered therefore the post-MS solution as being more
likely.
HIP 77015.The classification of this star as a Sco-Cen mem-
ber (UCL) by de Zeeuw et al. (1999) was later rejected by
Mamajek et al. (2002). Indeed, it is found to be a field ob-
ject in our kinematic analysis. Discrepant spectral classifi-
cation (G3V inHipparcos, G0.5V in Mamajek et al. 2002),
with photometric colors supporting the latter classification,
possibly even a slightly earlier one (F9.5/G0). The analysis
of the HARPS spectra by Grandjean et al. (2023) shows low
RV scatter (2.4 m/s for 15 spectra over 667 days) and low ac-
tivity. This is further supported by the X-ray non detection by
ROSAT. The EW(Li) from our analysis of HARPS spectra
(EW(Li)=77.1±4.4 mÅ) is intermediate between Hyades
and NGC 752 members of similar colors, suggestingt ∗ ∼
1 Gyr. Isochrones suggest an even older age (t⋆ =4±1 Gyr).
We concluded that the star is an old interloper.
HIP 92283=HD 174080.K3/K4 star from photometric col-
ors (Martínez-Arnáiz et al. 2010), in some tension with the
reported K0. The star appears to be older than the bulk
of SHINE stars. Kinematic space velocities are outside the
box typical of young stars (Montes et al. 2001). A moder-
ate chromospheric and coronal activity is observed, com-
patible but lower than the mean value of Hyades members
of similar colors. The nondetection of lithium adds only
limited constraints considering the late spectral type. There
is no measurement of rotation period; the ages expected
based on the logR ′
HK values by Duncan et al. (1991) and
Gomes da Silva et al. (2021) are 1.1 and 1.7 Gyr.
HIP 96085=HD 183870.K2V star forming a very wide bi-
nary system (ρ=208.6”∼3700 au) with the M5 star HD
183870B. A fast rotation period (2.32 d) andH α emission
were measured on the secondary by Pass et al. (2022), with
indication of an age older then 1 Gyr, while the rotation pe-
riod of the primary remains undetected. From the S index
measurement by Wright et al. (2004), an expected rotation
period of 18.7d was inferred, corresponding to a gyro-age of
1.3 Gyr. The X-ray and lithium non detection are compatible
with a moderately old star. We then adopted 1300±300 Myr.
C.3. Accreting or gas-rich
HIP 82323=AS 209.The star is very young, as shown by
the presence of a protoplanetary disk imaged by ALMA
(Andrews et al. 2018). Our analysis confirms an age<2 Myr.
The reddening is uncertain and likely very large (E(B−V)∼
1 mag). According toGaiaPMEX((S/N) RUWE)=3.7), an as-
trometric companion with unknownq– possibly extending
to planetary masses – exists; however, for such young stars,
spurious astrometric signals may arise due to accretion (see,
e.g., Lagrange et al. 2025a).
